# ARBORA-OPS requirements

| ID | Requirement | Verification |
|---|---|---|
| `ARBORA-OPS-R001` | **Native MCP/REST facade with A2A unary operations then streaming.** Arbora provides a native MCP and REST host. It fronts the engine and meristem services. It first exposes unary A2A operations: card, send, get, list, and cancel. It then adds durable streaming to those same handlers. | Integration tests exercise the A2A unary endpoints against a fixture engine, followed by a separate test proving durable streaming delivery. |
| `ARBORA-OPS-R002` | **Scoped A2A task routing and fleet tool exposure.** Arbora routes inbound A2A tasks to the correct operation handler and exposes, through its assembled multiplexer, only the caller-authorized subset of fleet tools, including typed work-item retrieval and listing bound by control leases and read classifications. | Integration tests assert inbound A2A tasks resolve to the correct handler and that multiplexer tool listings are filtered per caller. |
| `ARBORA-OPS-R003` | **Remove multiplexer skill/prompt harvest path.** Arbora removes the multiplexer's legacy skill and prompt harvesting and static hydration code path. Arbora sources skills and prompts only through the fleet discovery surface. | A regression test confirms the retired harvest module is absent and unimportable at runtime. |
| `ARBORA-OPS-R004` | **FastMCP 4 bridge for the MCP server.** The Arbora MCP bridge declares and runs on FastMCP 4. It provides native WebMCP support and a single consolidated event loop. Its own probes already exercise this version. | A semantic test suite plus a live probe confirm the deployed bridge reports FastMCP 4. |
| `ARBORA-OPS-R005` | **Stable auth error codes from the engine contract.** Arbora consumes the engine's packaged error-code contract. This includes the stable AUTH_TENANT_MISMATCH, AUTH_AUDIENCE_MISMATCH, and AUTH_POLICY_VERSION_MISMATCH codes. Arbora refuses to start if the installed contract is missing or inconsistent with its generated bindings. | A contract test fails closed when the packaged engine error contract is absent or mismatched against the generated bindings. |
| `ARBORA-OPS-R006` | **Consume the engine's packaged wire-callable contract.** Arbora reads the installed engine package's contract files for methods, errors, scopes, and JSON schemas. It binds operations only for engine methods marked wire-callable in that contract. | A package-contract test confirms the generated registry excludes any method not marked wire-callable in the installed engine contract. |
| `ARBORA-OPS-R007` | **Immutable operation registry with digest and authorization.** Arbora defines one immutable OpSpec registry with typed enums for verb, surface, effect, and scope, producing a canonical form and a stable SHA-256 registry digest, and evaluates per-call authorization at find time. | Unit tests in tests/api/test_registry.py cover canonical-form stability, digest reproducibility, and authorized-evaluation outcomes. |
| `ARBORA-OPS-R007.1` | **REST request authority.** Each `/api/v1` operation request runs under the verified session and actor that its own bearer resolved to. The REST surface binds that authority around the invocation chokepoint, as the MCP transport does per tool call. It never uses a process session or a synthesized one. An unauthenticated request answers 401 with `WWW-Authenticate: Bearer`. Proving test: `tests/api/test_http_session_scope.py`. |
| `ARBORA-OPS-R008` | **RETIRED: superseded by ARBORA-FLEET-R012.** `ARBORA-FLEET-R012` owns the generated engine-bound operations and their exclusions. | Not applicable. The bound evidence is on `ARBORA-FLEET-R012`. |
| `ARBORA-OPS-R009` | **Single invoke pipeline for authority, effect and audit.** Stage: every served operation. Caller: MCP (`arbora/api/mcp/serving.py`), HTTP (`arbora/api/http/routes.py`), A2A and `fleet.call` → `arbora/api/invoke/pipeline.py::invoke`. No other path reaches a handler. Order inside `invoke`, with the refusal code of each step: (1) registry lookup, `UNKNOWN_OP`. (2) `_static_refusal`: surface check `SURFACE_NOT_ALLOWED`, effect authority `UNAVAILABLE`. (3) `_arguments` through `validate_params` in `steps.py`, `INVALID_ARGUMENT`. (4) `_resolve` and `_bind_caller`: `authenticate` `UNAUTHENTICATED`, `principal_rule` `PRINCIPAL_NOT_ALLOWED`, `require_scopes` `SCOPE_REQUIRED`. (5) policy decision, `POLICY_DENIED` or `POLICY_UNAVAILABLE`. (6) subject read for service-executed ops, `SUBJECT_ACCESS_DENIED`. (7) `_confirmation_gate`: a destructive effect without `plan_ref` returns a preview. A plan bound to another operation, parameters, actor or policy revision returns `PLAN_MISMATCH`. A used or stale plan returns `PLAN_STALE`. A plan older than `PLAN_TTL_MS` (600000 ms, `Fixed`) returns `PLAN_EXPIRED`. (8) Admin and human-approval effects need a console session, a human principal and multi-factor authentication within `MFA_FRESH_SECONDS` (900 s, `Fixed`). Otherwise `STEP_UP_REQUIRED`. (9) audit reservation before the handler; a failed reservation stops the call. An outcome-link failure returns `INDETERMINATE` and keeps the reservation (`ARBORA-OPS-R036`). Service-bound operations run through `arbora/api/invoke/executor.py`. Writes: the audit reservation and the effect journal. Depends on: `ARBORA-OPS-R036`, `ARBORA-A2A-R007`. | Test: `tests/api/test_invoke_served.py::test_allow_deny_plan_expiry_and_step_up` (L3) drives HTTP and MCP through `invoke`, with a loopback medulla engine. It asserts one code per step above and identical results on both transports. |
| `ARBORA-OPS-R010` | **Closed error enum and one response envelope.** Arbora defines a closed error-code enum, including UNAUTHENTICATED, UNKNOWN_OP, NOT_FOUND, SCOPE_REQUIRED, and POLICY_DENIED, with a generated engine-error mapping. It converts every surface's response through one shared to_envelope function. | Unit tests check envelope shape and error-code mapping, and a generator diff check compares output against the packaged engine contract. |
| `ARBORA-OPS-R011` | **Six resident MCP verbs with typed discovery.** Arbora implements the six resident MCP verbs (find, ask, why, write, act, manage) through a shared verb constructor with typed input schemas, exposes registry entries as arbora:// resources, and includes the registry digest in the server's published instructions. | Unit tests in tests/api/test_mcp_verbs.py cover verb schema validation, resource listing, and digest presence. |
| `ARBORA-OPS-R012` | **Bounded resolver for natural-language operation lookup.** Arbora resolves natural-language and partial queries to registry operations using combined lexical and semantic ranking over generated descriptors, a tenant- and policy-partitioned outcome moving average, and a bounded result cache, falling any unmatched ask call back to a read-only natural-language query preview rather than a direct mutation. | Unit tests in tests/api/test_resolve.py cover ranking order, cache bounds, and the preview-only fallback behavior. |
| `ARBORA-OPS-R013` | **Generated HTTP API for every registry operation.** Arbora exposes each registry operation at POST /api/v1/ops/{op_id}. It also exposes generated resource routes on a dedicated sub-application. The sub-application has an authentication dependency and CSRF protection for cookie sessions. It also has cursor pagination, idempotency-key handling, and discovery endpoints for the registry and OpenAPI document. | Integration tests in tests/api/test_http_*.py cover routing, authentication, CSRF enforcement, and pagination and idempotency behavior. |
| `ARBORA-OPS-R014` | **RETIRED: identity operations have one owner.** `ARBORA-IDENTITY-R018` owns the identity operations and `ARBORA-IDENTITY-R025` owns their registration. Frozen child `R014.1` keeps its ID. | Retired; see ARBORA-IDENTITY-R018. |
| `ARBORA-OPS-R015` | **RETIRED: access operations have one owner.** `ARBORA-IDENTITY-R019` owns the access operations. The operations already exist in `arbora/api/ops/access.py`. | Retired; see ARBORA-IDENTITY-R019. |
| `ARBORA-OPS-R016` | **RETIRED: capacity operations have one owner.** `ARBORA-CAPACITY-R002` owns `capacity.throttle.status` and `capacity.throttle.set_mode` in `arbora/api/ops/capacity.py`. | Retired; see ARBORA-CAPACITY-R002. |
| `ARBORA-OPS-R017` | **Finance and markets operations with tenant isolation (rollup).** Stage: served operations `markets.*`, `events.*`, `orders.*` and the position and paper-trading actions. Caller: MCP or HTTP → `invoke` → `arbora/api/ops/finance.py` handlers → `arbora/finance/markets.py::get_market_read` (provider port) and the medulla `Finance*` methods. Ownership split: Arbora serves the operations and holds approvals. Medulla computes every figure natively (`MEDULLA-FINANCE-PRIMITIVES`). `SCION-FINANCE-SOURCES` defines the source contract. `emerald-exchange` holds only venue calls and order routing. Reads: `ARBORA-OPS-R017.1` and `ARBORA-DATA-MARKET-R003`. Confirmed order and position changes: `ARBORA-DATA-MARKET-R007`. Subject ownership: `ARBORA-OPS-R017.2`. Writes: none by the rollup. Depends on: R017.1, R017.2, `ARBORA-DATA-MARKET-R003`, `ARBORA-DATA-MARKET-R007`. | Root test: `tests/api/test_finance_served.py::test_two_tenants_never_cross` (L3) reads as two tenants over HTTP and MCP through `invoke`, with a loopback medulla engine. No cross-tenant data may appear. |
| `ARBORA-OPS-R017.1` | **Tenant-scoped market reads under finance:read.** Arbora executes market reads server-side through the service executor under the finance:read scope, refusing with zero provider calls on a missing or mismatched tenant/account binding. | Tests confirm a matching binding reaches the provider and a missing or cross-tenant binding refuses before any provider call. |
| `ARBORA-OPS-R017.2` | **Position and paper-trading actions with subject ownership checks.** Stage: served write operations `positions.*` and `orders.paper_submit`. Caller: `invoke` → `arbora/api/invoke/executor.py::prepare_executor` → `runtime.check_subject_access(caller, subject)` → handler in `arbora/api/ops/finance.py`. Decision: the subject check already exists in the pipeline. The earlier block on a later engine contract is void. Rule: each operation declares `executor=Executor.SERVICE`, `principals=PrincipalRule.HUMAN_UNDELEGATED`, `subject=SubjectRef(source=SubjectSource.PARAM, path='params.account_id')`, scopes `finance:read` plus `finance:paper-trade` and `executor_scopes` `finance:paper-trade`. `prepare_executor` resolves the account id and asks `check_subject_access`. A caller who does not own the account gets `SUBJECT_ACCESS_DENIED`. A request that carries an owner field gets `INVALID_ARGUMENT` (`FORBIDDEN_OWNER`). Arbora calls the provider only after the check passes. A failed check makes zero provider calls and writes no audit effect. Config: none. Writes: the paper order through the venue tool and the audit reservation. Depends on: `ARBORA-DATA-MARKET-R003.5`, `ARBORA-DATA-MARKET-R007`, `ARBORA-OPS-R009`. | Test: `tests/api/test_finance_served.py::test_paper_action_requires_account_ownership` (L3) runs HTTP and MCP through `invoke`, with a loopback medulla engine. It asserts the owner succeeds once. It asserts another tenant gets `SUBJECT_ACCESS_DENIED` and a controlled provider records zero calls. |
| `ARBORA-OPS-R018` | **Query, search, ontology and analytics operations (rollup).** Stage: served read operations over the caller's graph. Caller: MCP or HTTP → `invoke` → `EgMethod` binding → the hand-written medulla client under the caller's verified claims. Curated operations exist in `arbora/api/ops/query.py` (`query.uql`, `query.nl`), `query_engine.py` (`query.sparql`, `query.sql`, `ontology.inspect`, `ontology.schema.classes`, `ontology.validate`, `federation.sources.columns`) and `retrieval.py`. Every other engine method is served as the generated operation `eg.<service>.<Method>` (`load_eg_bindings`). Arbora adds no local ontology store and no local planner. Operations pass parameters as typed values; they never interpolate SQL text. Writes: none. Children: `.1` ontology and schema, `.2` query, search, indexes and series. Depends on: `MEDULLA-FEDERATED-QUERY`, `ARBORA-OPS-R009`. | Root test: `tests/api/test_query_served.py::test_each_query_family_reaches_engine_with_tenant_and_budget` (L3) runs HTTP and MCP through `invoke`, with a loopback medulla engine. |
| `ARBORA-OPS-R018.1` | **Sparql, schema and ontology operations.** Stage: served read operations. Caller: `invoke` → `arbora/api/ops/query_engine.py::operations` bindings. Rule: the supported set is `query.sparql` (`Method::Sparql`), `ontology.inspect` (`OntologyInspect`), `ontology.schema.classes` (`GraphSchemaClasses`), `ontology.schema.graph` (`GraphSchema`), `ontology.schema.list` (`GraphSchemaList`) and `ontology.validate` (`ShaclValidate`). Add `ontology.schema.graph` and `ontology.schema.list` to `query_engine.py`, each with scope `owl:read`. SQL table and column discovery for the caller's graph is `ontology.schema.list`. A foreign source's columns stay `federation.sources.columns`. Decision: the ontology operations without an engine method are not curated. Loading triples is `eg.<service>.AddTriples`. Catalogue listing is `eg.<service>.CatalogList`. Export is `query.sparql` with a CONSTRUCT query. Linting is `ontology.validate`. Generation belongs to the SDK ontology pack generator (`SCION-CONNECTOR-CONTROL`), not Arbora. Each operation takes the graph from the verified session, never from the request. Config: none. Writes: none. Depends on: `MEDULLA-FEDERATED-QUERY`. | Test: `tests/api/test_query_ops.py::test_ontology_schema_graph_and_list_bind_engine_methods` (L1). Test: `tests/api/test_query_served.py::test_sparql_and_schema_routes_reach_engine` (L3) runs HTTP and MCP through `invoke`, with a loopback medulla engine. |
| `ARBORA-OPS-R018.2` | **Uql, sql, search, index and series operations.** Stage: served read operations. Caller: `invoke` → `EgMethod` binding or the retrieval port. Rule: `query.uql` binds `Uql`. `query.sql` binds `Sql`. `query.nl` binds `NlQuery`. Search is `retrieval.search` (`ARBORA-OPS-R021.2.1`). Index operations are the generated `eg.<service>.SemanticIndex` and `SemanticSearch`. Series operations are the generated `eg.<service>.TsRange`, `TsWindow` and `TsListSeries`. These need no curated declaration. Every operation runs under the caller's tenant. Arbora forwards the caller budget unchanged (R018.2.2). Config: none. Writes: none. Depends on: `ARBORA-OPS-R018.2.2`, `MEDULLA-FEDERATED-QUERY`. | Test: `tests/api/test_query_served.py::test_uql_sql_series_and_index_ops_reach_engine` (L3) runs HTTP and MCP through `invoke`, with a loopback medulla engine. |
| `ARBORA-OPS-R018.2.1` | **Distinct caller-bound query contracts.** `query.uql` binds EG `Uql` under `query:unified`, using the provider inner request-parameter schema. `query.nl` binds EG `NlQuery` under `query:nl`, accepts text only, and obtains its graph from the current verified caller session. Both preserve provider result and refusal contracts. Missing NL feature/planner refuses at the provider; there is no local planner, scope substitution, or automatic grant. | Binding, resolver and dispatch regressions prove distinct scopes, strict inputs, caller graph binding, and provider refusal propagation without deterministic fallback. |
| `ARBORA-OPS-R018.2.2` | **Query serving acceptance, budgets and planner refusal.** Stage: served read operations, acceptance slice. Caller: `invoke` → `query.nl` handler `arbora/api/ops/query_federation.py::dispatch_federated_nl` and the `EgMethod` bindings. Rule: compose provider schema validation (`_validate_provider` in `steps.py`). Qualify caller-bound queries, tenant limits, scope checks and the unavailable planner refusal through both transports. Arbora forwards the caller's budget unchanged. It never clamps, defaults or widens it. The engine is the budget authority and clamps to the tenant ceiling (`MEDULLA-DECISION-ENGINE-R144.7`). Arbora returns the applied budget from the engine `CostReport` as served. Refusals: no composed planner returns `UNAVAILABLE` with no fallback to local SQL. A missing scope returns `SCOPE_REQUIRED` before any engine call. Declaration and dispatcher tests (R018.2.1) do not qualify this runtime behaviour. Writes: none. Depends on: R018.2, `MEDULLA-DECISION-ENGINE-R144.7`. | Test: `tests/api/test_query_served.py::test_over_ceiling_budget_reaches_engine_unchanged` (L3) asserts the engine received the caller budget and the response shows the applied one. Test: `tests/api/test_query_served.py::test_unavailable_planner_refuses_without_fallback` (L3). |
| `ARBORA-OPS-R019` | **Federation source operations (rollup).** Stage: served operations `federation.sources.*`. Caller: `invoke` → `arbora/api/ops/federation.py::handle_federation` → port `federation_sources` → medulla source catalog (`ForeignSourceCatalog`, `MEDULLA-UNIFIED-DATA-PLANE-R002`, `R612`). Decision: one engine, one record. The durable source record, its owner and its grantees live in medulla under the sharing enum of `MEDULLA-IDENTITY-R610`. Arbora holds no catalog. The port has no production composition today, so every operation returns `UNAVAILABLE`. R019.1 adds the composition. Registration is a governed write (`federation.sources.register`, `query_engine.py`). Probe results redact credentials. Children: `.1` register, list, get and probe. `.2` share and unshare. Depends on: `MEDULLA-UNIFIED-DATA-PLANE-R765`, `MEDULLA-IDENTITY-R610`. | Root test: `tests/api/test_federation_served.py::test_share_probe_and_revoke_over_both_transports` (L3) runs HTTP and MCP through `invoke`, with a loopback medulla engine. |
| `ARBORA-OPS-R019.1` | **Federation source register, list, get and probe.** Stage: served operations. Caller: `invoke` → `handle_federation` → `EngineFederationCatalog` in new `arbora/federation/engine_catalog.py`, composed as service `federation_sources` in `arbora/mcp_server/server.py`. The adapter holds no state. It calls the medulla catalog methods added by the new row of R019 under the caller's claims. Rule: remove `_can_see` from `federation.py`; the engine enforces visibility. Keep `redact` as a second guard on every result. `federation.sources.register` stays the governed write in `query_engine.py` (`Effect.ADMIN`, idempotency key required, human principal). Refusals: an uncomposed adapter returns `UNAVAILABLE`. An invisible source returns `NOT_FOUND` with no hint of existence. Writes: registration through `RegisterForeignSource`. Depends on: R019, `ARBORA-OPS-R009`. | Test: `tests/api/test_federation_ops.py::test_adapter_calls_engine_and_redacts` (L1). Test: `tests/api/test_federation_served.py::test_register_list_get_probe_redact_credentials` (L3) runs HTTP and MCP through `invoke`, with a loopback medulla engine. |
| `ARBORA-OPS-R019.2` | **Federation source share and unshare.** Stage: served governed writes. Caller: `invoke` → `handle_federation` → `EngineFederationCatalog.grant` and `revoke` → `ShareForeignSource` and `UnshareForeignSource` (added by the new row of R019). Rule: both operations declare scope `federation:admin`, `Effect.ADMIN` and an idempotency key. The engine decides ownership; Arbora keeps no owner check. A replay with the same key returns the same receipt. Revocation takes effect for the grantee's next probe. Refusals: a non-owner gets `SOURCE_OWNER_REQUIRED`. A missing key gets `INVALID_ARGUMENT`. Writes: the grant edge in the engine. Depends on: R019.1. | Test: `tests/api/test_federation_served.py::test_only_owner_shares_and_revocation_blocks_probe` (L3) runs HTTP and MCP through `invoke`, with a loopback medulla engine. It asserts the stranger never sees the source. |
| `ARBORA-OPS-R020` | **Ingest operations through a typed runner facade (rollup).** Stage: served operations `ingest.*`. Caller: MCP or HTTP → `invoke` → `arbora/api/ops/ingest.py` → `arbora/ingest/service.py` handlers → port `ingest_runner`. The port is `ConnectorIngestRunner` in `arbora/ingest/connector_sources.py`. It reaches the SDK source runner and the medulla `SourceIngest` method. Operations: repository indexing, source sync, pack and job management, drift listing and repair with approval, embedding admission and re-embedding. Rule: handlers call only the typed port. A missing runner returns `UNAVAILABLE` and no operation falls back to a local path. A sync returns a durable job receipt. The WorkItem authority of the engine owns the idempotency of `ingest.sources.sync` (`NATIVE_IDEMPOTENCY`). Writes: jobs and receipts in the engine. Children: `.1` to `.3` and `.6` landed, `.4` drift, `.5` embeddings. Depends on: `SCION-SOURCE-INGEST`, `MEDULLA-INGEST-WORKER`. | Root test: `tests/api/test_ingest_served.py::test_sync_returns_durable_receipt_and_absent_runner_fails_closed` (L3) runs HTTP and MCP through `invoke`, with a loopback medulla engine. |
| `ARBORA-OPS-R020.1` | **Typed ingest runner port and the source-sync entry point.** Arbora defines one typed port for all ingestion SDK calls. It exposes ingest.sources.sync. If no runner is composed, it fails closed with UNAVAILABLE. | A test proves a durable job receipt on success and a typed UNAVAILABLE refusal when the runner is absent. |
| `ARBORA-OPS-R020.2` | **Repository indexing and source inventory operations.** Arbora exposes `ingest.repositories.index` and `ingest.sources.{list,status}` through the same typed runner port. | Integration tests confirm each operation reaches the runner and fails closed with `UNAVAILABLE` when the runner is absent. |
| `ARBORA-OPS-R020.3` | **Pack and job management operations.** Arbora exposes `ingest.packs.*` and `ingest.jobs.*` through the same typed runner port. | Integration tests confirm pack and job operations reach the runner and report durable job state. |
| `ARBORA-OPS-R020.3.1` | **Missing ingest.packs.* and ingest.jobs.* ops.** This row rolls up ARBORA-OPS-R020.3.1.1 through ARBORA-OPS-R020.3.1.2. No ingest.packs.* or ingest.jobs.* operation exists through the typed runner port. | Integration tests confirm ingest.packs.* and ingest.jobs.* reach the runner and report durable job state. They also confirm UNAVAILABLE when the runner is absent. |
| `ARBORA-OPS-R020.3.1.1` | **`ingest.packs.list` op.** Arbora exposes `ingest.packs.list` as a read operation through the typed ingest runner port, listing this tenant's ingestion packs. | Tests confirm the operation reaches the runner with the caller's tenant and declares the existing `ingest:read` scope. They also require fail-closed `UNAVAILABLE` when the runner is absent. |
| `ARBORA-OPS-R020.3.1.2` | **`ingest.jobs.status` op.** Arbora exposes `ingest.jobs.status` as a read operation through the typed ingest runner port. It reports the state of one durable job. Depends on `ARBORA-OPS-R020.3.1.1` for the shared pack/job port conventions. | Tests confirm the op reaches the runner, reports durable job state and declares `ingest:read`. It fails closed with `UNAVAILABLE` when the runner is absent. |
| `ARBORA-OPS-R020.4` | **Drift listing and repair-with-approval operations.** Stage: served operations `ingest.drift.list`, `ingest.drift.get`, `ingest.drift.repair_propose` and `ingest.drift.repair_approve`. Caller: `invoke` → `arbora/ingest/service.py::{list_drift,get_drift,propose_repair,approve_repair}` → `ConnectorIngestRunner` methods of the same names → medulla `SchemaDriftList`, `SchemaDriftGet`, `SchemaRepairPropose` and `SchemaRepairApprove` (`MEDULLA-TYPED-PACKS-R806`). Drift detection and classification stay in the engine (`MEDULLA-TYPED-PACKS-R710`, `R714`). The SDK records the proposal and pauses the stream (`SCION-SOURCE-INGEST-R003`). Arbora serves and gates. Rule: `repair_propose` records a preview and applies nothing. `repair_approve` declares `Confirm.PLAN`, takes `plan_ref` and `approval_ref`, and applies only the previewed repair. The `approval_ref` is a two-person approval receipt from the Arbora approval flow (`ARBORA-OPS-R863` pattern). The proposer cannot approve. Refusals: `PLAN_MISMATCH` for a changed repair, `POLICY_DENIED` for a missing approval, `UNAVAILABLE` for an absent runner. Writes: the repair activation in the engine and the audit reservation. Depends on: `ARBORA-OPS-R009`, `MEDULLA-TYPED-PACKS-R806`. | Test: `tests/api/test_ingest_drift_embedding_ops.py::test_drift_ops_call_runner_with_tenant` (L1). Test: `tests/api/test_ingest_served.py::test_drift_repair_previews_then_applies_with_approval` (L3) runs HTTP and MCP through `invoke`, with a loopback medulla engine. |
| `ARBORA-OPS-R020.5` | **Embedding admission and re-embedding operations.** Stage: served operations `ingest.embedding.admit` and `ingest.embedding.reembed`. Caller: `invoke` → `arbora/ingest/service.py::{admit_embedding,reembed}` → `ConnectorIngestRunner.admit_embedding` and `reembed` → medulla `EmbeddingAdmit` and `Reembed` (`MEDULLA-FEDERATED-QUERY-R723`). Rule: the engine owns the one re-embed executor (`src/server/handlers/semantic_index/worker.rs`, `MEDULLA-FEDERATED-QUERY-R039.2`). Arbora starts a job and never embeds. Both operations declare `Effect.WRITE` and require an idempotency key. They return the durable job id and state. A re-embed names one source and one admitted model revision. An unadmitted model returns `EMBEDDING_MODEL_NOT_ADMITTED`. Refusals: `UNAVAILABLE` for an absent runner, `INVALID_ARGUMENT` for a missing key. Writes: the job record in the engine. Depends on: `ARBORA-OPS-R020.4`, `MEDULLA-FEDERATED-QUERY-R723`. | Test: `tests/api/test_ingest_drift_embedding_ops.py::test_embedding_ops_call_runner_and_return_job_state` (L1). Test: `tests/api/test_ingest_served.py::test_reembed_creates_one_durable_job_per_key` (L3) runs HTTP and MCP through `invoke`, with a loopback medulla engine. |
| `ARBORA-OPS-R020.6` | **Connector sources served through the composed SDK sync runner.** Arbora composes the SDK source sync runner as the `ingest_runner` service. `ingest.sources.list`, `ingest.sources.sync` and `ingest.sources.status` then serve connector sources end to end. The SDK owns the declarations and the runner (SCION-SOURCE-INGEST-R004.19 through SCION-SOURCE-INGEST-R004.24). Delivered as the rollup of `ARBORA-OPS-R020.6.1` through `ARBORA-OPS-R020.6.7`. | The rollup closes when `ARBORA-OPS-R020.6.1` through `ARBORA-OPS-R020.6.7` pass their proving tests. |
| `ARBORA-OPS-R020.6.1` | **Model.** `ConnectorIngestRunner` in `arbora/ingest/connector_sources.py` implements the ingest runner port for connector sources. A source ID is `<server>/<source>`. `IngestSourceRecord` adds the connector, record type, incremental flag, cursor, record count and last job ID. | `tests/ingest/test_connector_sources.py::test_source_record_shape` validates the record and refuses a malformed source ID. |

Engine codes (`APPROVAL_MISSING`, `APPROVAL_STALE`) pass through unchanged. Writes: `DecisionCommit` only. Refusal: Unknown runs and other tenants must be indistinguishable; missing fields must remain unavailable. | Evidence: planned `tests/api/test_runs_ops.py::test_runs_get_returns_termination_context_and_applied_config` plus the served cases in test-spec.md. |
| `ARBORA-OPS-R021.4.5.1` | **Current-plan read boundary.** Model: SDK-owned `CurrentPlanRequest`, `PlanItem`, `CurrentPlanSnapshot` and `CurrentPlanRead` from `agent_connector_sdk.current_plan` (SCION-CONNECTOR-CONTROL-R070). Arbora imports these values from a published SDK distribution that includes that contract. Each model refuses unknown fields and cannot change. A snapshot holds the run ID and a revision of zero or more. It also holds UTC observation and expiry times and ordered items. Each item has a status of `pending`, `in_progress` or `done`. An available empty plan differs from an unavailable read. Behavior: `runs.current_plan` reads the plan of one run through the injected `current_plan_reader` port. It passes the admitted execution context to the port; the verified caller supplies tenant and principal. Arbora stores no plan and does not rebuild a plan from stored messages. Wiring: `handle_runs_current_plan` uses `build_read_op` and the existing `kg:read` scope. `arbora/api/ops/registry_factory.py` lists `runs`. `runs.get` reuses `read_current_plan` for its `current_plan` field. Refusal: An absent port, an unknown run or a run of another caller returns the same unavailable read. It holds no items. A malformed result, a result for a different run, a future observation time or an expired snapshot also return unavailable. Parameters cannot supply caller authority. | `tests/api/test_runs_ops.py` covers caller forwarding, item order, the absent port, malformed parameters, malformed snapshots and each fail-closed projection. |
| `ARBORA-OPS-R021.4.5.1.1` | **Native contextual reader composition.** The served invocation root binds a stateless current-plan reader. Each read rechecks the admitted caller session, owner and graph, preserves `kg:read`, and sends generated EG `GetCurrentPlan` under the verified caller claims. EG still requires `work:read`; Arbora never widens scopes or substitutes service authority. Map native UTC milliseconds and ordered items into SDK values, retaining unavailable/future/expiry projection. An installed package without the method returns unavailable. No plan store or producer admission is introduced. | Contextual reader and serving-root tests cover caller isolation, authority refusal, missing method, native null and malformed/expired snapshots. Installed native acceptance remains pending the published EG contract containing #663. |

The binding uses the exact verified session, tenant and canonical control graph. It also uses the exact holder, epoch, fence and expiry. Publish actual AU revisions through EG and route host-managed current-plan reads to the same graph. Each timing must name a `turn_receipt_ref` present in the same result. Arbora passes the observations through beside the usage receipt. Client elapsed time is not provider generation time, queue time or token rate. The schema holds no such field. The Rust serde types of MEDULLA-DURABLE-KERNEL-R085.1 are the only source of truth. Rule: `MemorySyncOutcome` is a tagged union. The accepted variant carries head fields. The refused variant carries none. Every model forbids extra fields. Digests are SHA-256 with 64 lowercase hex characters. Refusals: `INVALID_ARGUMENT` on a field-validation failure. Evidence: none. | `ARBORA-OPS-R024.4.2` | **Snapshot reads and listing.** Stage: arbora served operations `memory.files.read` and `memory.files.list`. Caller: `POST /api/v1/ops/memory.files.read` or the MCP verb. Next call: `_send`. Next call: `Method::ReadMemorySnapshot`. For `memory.files.list` the next call is `Method::ListMemorySnapshots`. Code: add both handlers and OpSpecs to `operations()` in `arbora/api/ops/memory.py`, built with `build_read_op`, scope `memory:read`. Rule: pass the cursor and filters through unchanged. The page size is the request value clamped to `[1, 200]`. Refusals: an expired or wrong-scope cursor returns the engine's `CURSOR_EXPIRED` or `SUBJECT_ACCESS_DENIED` unchanged. Other read and list refusals carry the engine `MEMSYNC_*` codes unchanged. Responses carry no hidden counts or identifiers. Evidence: audit record with the document identifier digest. Writes: none. Depends on: MEDULLA-DURABLE-KERNEL-R085.2, ARBORA-OPS-R024.4.1. | Test: `tests/api/test_memory_files_served.py::test_read_list_preserve_scope_and_cursor` (L3). It asserts scope, cursor binding, an expired cursor refusal and one cross-tenant negative case. |
| `ARBORA-OPS-R024.4.3` | **Versioned saves and conflicts.** Stage: arbora served operation `memory.files.save`. Caller: `POST /api/v1/ops/memory.files.save` or the MCP verb. Next call: `make_endpoint` in `arbora/api/http/routes.py`. Next call: `invoke` in `arbora/api/invoke/pipeline.py`. Next call: `handle_memory_files_save` in `arbora/api/ops/memory.py`. Next call: `_send`. Next call: `Method::SyncMemorySnapshot` with action `submit` or `resolve`. Code: add the handler and a write OpSpec built with `build_write_op`, scope `memory:write`, idempotency `KEY_REQUIRED`. Rule: forward the base revision precondition, bytes, digest and idempotency key unchanged. Arbora never merges. A stale authorized save returns the engine's `conflict_retained` outcome with both versions kept. Refusals: a refused save returns the redacted variant with no head fields. Digest mismatch returns `INVALID_ARGUMENT` before dispatch. Evidence: audit record with request identity and content digest. Writes: through `SyncMemorySnapshot` only. The outcomes are `Accepted`, `ConflictRetained` and `Resolved` (`MEDULLA-DURABLE-KERNEL-R085.3`). `MEMSYNC_NOT_AVAILABLE` maps to the single denial `SUBJECT_ACCESS_DENIED`. Depends on: MEDULLA-DURABLE-KERNEL-R085.3, ARBORA-OPS-R024.4.1. | Test: `tests/api/test_memory_files_served.py::test_competing_saves_preserve_both_versions` (L3). It asserts both versions kept, resolution once and a replay returning the first outcome. |

Caller: `compose_served_invocation` in `arbora/mcp_server/served_invocation.py` → `MemoryMaterializer.start` → `materialize`. Code: new `MemoryMaterializer` in `arbora/api/ops/memory_files.py`, built beside `build_operation_runtime` in `arbora/api/invoke/bootstrap.py`. Rule: after the engine accepts a head, write the projection to the hosted directory. Then send the action `acknowledge` with the exact head revision, digest and observation generation. Configuration: key `memory.files_root` in `~/.config/arbora/config.json`, no default, required when `memory.files.*` is registered. Refusals: a failed file write sends no acknowledgment. A delayed acknowledgment for an older generation is ignored by the engine and never clears a newer observation or conflict. Evidence: acknowledgment carries head and generation. Writes: hosted files only. Engine state changes through `SyncMemorySnapshot`. Depends on: MEDULLA-DURABLE-KERNEL-R085.6. | Test: `tests/api/test_memory_files_served.py::test_materialization_ack_checks_generation` (L3). It asserts no acknowledgment after a failed write and no clearing by a stale acknowledgment. |

This row adds the memory file operations to `tests/api/authority_matrix.yaml`. Rule: a denied request for an existing document, an absent document and a creation attempt return one identical refusal. A replay with a changed body returns `IDEMPOTENCY_CONFLICT` with no effect. Refusals: `SUBJECT_ACCESS_DENIED` (identical in all three cases), `IDEMPOTENCY_CONFLICT`. Evidence: audit record without document existence. Writes: none beyond ARBORA-OPS-R024.4.3. Depends on: MEDULLA-DURABLE-KERNEL-R085.5, ARBORA-OPS-R024.4.2. | Test: `tests/api/test_memory_files_served.py::test_authority_and_replay_are_non_disclosing` (L3). It compares response bytes for the three denied cases and asserts zero effects on a changed-body replay. |
| `ARBORA-OPS-R024.4.6` | **Registration and served proof.** Stage: arbora registry. Code: register the three file operations in `arbora/api/ops/memory.py::operations`; `get_registry` in `arbora/api/ops/registry_factory.py` already includes the memory module. Schemas generate under ARBORA-OPS-R025. Rule: the operations stay unregistered until the engine reports the three methods. The registration check reads the engine method list and omits them otherwise. Real HTTP and MCP calls traverse `invoke` and durable engine dispatch. Refusals: `UNAVAILABLE` when a method is missing. No test replaces the host, gateway or engine transport. Evidence: the registry digest changes when the operations register. Writes: none. It runs read, list, competing saves, resolution and acknowledgment over HTTP and MCP against a loopback engine. |
| `ARBORA-OPS-R844` | **Served memory read and write over EG KV.** Extends `ARBORA-OPS-R024.4`. `memory.read` maps to EG `KvGet` and `memory.write` to EG `KvPut`. A write with `expected_version` maps to EG `KvCas`; `0` means only when absent. Each call runs under the caller's verified session, so entries are per-principal by default. A key is stored at `memory/<namespace>/<key>`: EG namespace `memory`, EG key `<namespace>/<key>`, default namespace `default`. Every result returns the entry version. An absent key reads as `found: false`. A compare-and-set mismatch returns `swapped: false` with the current version. Writes need an idempotency key, which EG replays. Scopes: `kv:read` and `kv:write`. | `tests/test_served_operation_api.py::test_served_memory_operations_round_trip_over_eg_kv` |
| `ARBORA-OPS-R845` | **Served memory list and delete.** Extends `ARBORA-OPS-R024.4`. `memory.list` maps to EG `KvScan` over one namespace, with an optional key prefix, cursor and limit up to 200. Listed keys are returned without the namespace prefix. `memory.delete` maps to EG `KvDelete` and reports whether the entry existed. | `tests/test_served_operation_api.py::test_served_memory_operations_round_trip_over_eg_kv` |
| `ARBORA-OPS-R846` | **Explicit shared memory scope.** Extends `ARBORA-OPS-R024.4`. `scope: "shared"` selects EG's shared KV namespace. A shared write or delete also needs `kv:shared:write`. Without it Arbora refuses with `SCOPE_REQUIRED` and names the missing scope, and sends nothing to EG. | `tests/test_served_operation_api.py::test_served_memory_operations_round_trip_over_eg_kv` |
| `ARBORA-OPS-R847` | **KV methods in the registry.** Extends `ARBORA-OPS-R006`. Arbora requires EG 2.27.3 or later. The registry carries `eg.storage.KvGet`, `KvPut`, `KvScan`, `KvDelete` and `KvCas` from the installed contract, beside `memory.read`, `memory.write`, `memory.list` and `memory.delete`. The web UI's `memory.read`, `memory.write` and `memory.delete` calls resolve to these operations. | The in-code registry lists the KV and memory operations; `tests/test_served_operation_api.py` calls them over HTTP. |
| `ARBORA-OPS-R024.5` | **Operational admin operations.** Stage: arbora served operations `ops.backup` and `ops.restore` (`Effect.ADMIN`, scope `ops:admin`); `ops.doctor`, `ops.config` and `ops.tenants` are reads. Caller: `POST /api/v1/ops/ops.backup` or the MCP verb → `make_endpoint` in `arbora/api/http/routes.py` → `invoke` in `arbora/api/invoke/pipeline.py` → `handle_ops_backup` in `arbora/api/ops/ops.py`. Rule: `invoke` returns `STEP_UP_REQUIRED` (HTTP 428) unless the caller holds console step-up and an undelegated human session (`_admin_authority` in `arbora/api/ops/_common.py`). A plain confirmation never satisfies it. The pre-dispatch audit reservation of ARBORA-OPS-R036 precedes every effect. Tenant creation and suspension belong to ARBORA-IDENTITY. Graph, query and ontology administration belong to ARBORA-HOST-R026. Refusals: `STEP_UP_REQUIRED`, `SCOPE_REQUIRED`, `UNAVAILABLE` without a composed backup port. | `ARBORA-OPS-R025` | **Registry in code is the API contract.** The operation registry built by `arbora/api/ops/registry_factory.py` is the only source of operation IDs, schemas and the registry digest. No generator runs and no generated contract file is committed. | `tests/api/test_client_http.py` and the served-operation tests exercise the in-code registry directly. |

Matching envelopes pass through unchanged. | Client tests prove unknown operations make zero calls and mismatched digests are refused. |
| `ARBORA-OPS-R025.2` | RETIRED: generated contract artifacts were removed; the in-code registry is the contract (ARBORA-OPS-R025). | — |

The pre-commit hook runs them on every commit. |

| `ARBORA-OPS-R028` | **Exported domain scope classes.** Arbora defines and exports its domain scope classes, including finance:read, fleet:read, fleet:control, loops:read, loops:control, ops:read, and ops:admin, for consumption by the engine's scope registry. | Unit tests confirm each exported scope triple matches its declared class and is consumed without duplication. |

| `ARBORA-OPS-R031` | **Published tool-to-operation parity inventory.** Arbora publishes a complete parity inventory mapping every former hosted intent verb and tool to its destination operation or a documented intentional drop, so that a consuming agent project can remove its legacy intent-tool definitions only once that inventory and its generated client are published. | A parity-inventory test confirms full coverage of former tool names against the published mapping. |

Children: `.1` import gate, `.2` composition root, `.2.2` mcp_server, `.2.3` webui_host, `.2.4` single-runtime proof. | `ARBORA-OPS-R033` | **Dynamic multiplexer discovery, load, and call.** Arbora implements find_tools (with browse=true replacing the former catalog listing), load_tools, unload_tools, and multiplexer_status over one per-caller-filtered catalog spanning fleet tools, prompts, resources, skills, and connector items, enforcing a default load cap of 64 (hard maximum 256), one-hour idle expiry, opt-in LRU eviction, and routing every loaded tool's body through a fleet call with the caller's delegated credential. | Integration tests confirm the load cap, idle expiry, and that each loaded tool call is dispatched through the fleet-call path rather than directly. |
| `ARBORA-OPS-R034` | **Eunomia narrowing-only policy enforcement.** Arbora applies its policy engine as a narrowing-only step at discovery, load, and every call, always leaving exact-scope filtering in place when policy is off, failing closed when policy is enabled but unreachable, defaulting to on for local and external deployments and off only for the none deployment mode, with its doctor check warning when policy is disabled. | Integration tests cover the off, on, and unreachable policy states. A doctor-check test confirms the warning fires when policy is off. |
| `ARBORA-OPS-R035` | **Multiplexer registration reduced to four resident tools.** Stage: arbora MCP surface. The resident surface is the six intent verbs plus `find_tools`, `load_tools`, `unload_tools` and `multiplexer_status`, mapped by `FLEET_OPERATIONS` in `arbora/api/mcp/registration.py`. Caller: `mcp_server()` → `create_mcp_server` in `arbora/mcp_server/backing/server_factory.py` → `register_mcp_tools` → `GovernedSessionVisibility`. Rule: the middleware only filters `on_list_tools` to resident names plus the caller's loaded items. It raises no denial. Authorization happens only in `invoke` in `arbora/api/invoke/pipeline.py`. Children: `.1` unit proof, `.2` live wiring. Depends on: ARBORA-OPS-R029. | Root L3 journey: `tests/api/test_resident_tool_surface.py::test_running_server_lists_exactly_the_resident_tools` (L3). |
| `ARBORA-OPS-R035.1` | **Four-tool meta-registration and routing-only visibility, unit-bound.** Arbora's prepared multiplexer registration module defines exactly the four resident fleet meta-tools (find_tools, load_tools, unload_tools, multiplexer_status), and its GovernedSessionVisibility middleware only routes the list-tools candidate set (resident names plus the caller's currently loaded items), raising no denial of its own. | A test confirms FLEET_OPERATIONS is exactly the four named tools. GovernedSessionVisibility.on_list_tools filters a candidate tool list to resident-plus-loaded without raising. |
| `ARBORA-OPS-R035.2` | **Registration wired into the live server.** Stage: arbora MCP startup. Caller: `mcp_server()` in `arbora/mcp_server/server.py` → `create_mcp_server` → `register_mcp_tools(mcp, multiplexer, binding)` in `arbora/api/mcp/registration.py`. Today `register_intent_surface` in `arbora/api/mcp/serving.py` registers the six verbs and nothing calls `register_mcp_tools`. This row creates the call site. Rule: startup calls `register_mcp_tools` once, after the legacy tools are retired and live authorities validate. It replaces the `register_intent_surface` verb loop. The MCP Apps launchers stay registered. An attachment failure aborts startup. Refusals: `RuntimeError('MCP surface must be empty and unbound before registration')` when the surface is not empty. Evidence: none. Writes: none. Depends on: ARBORA-OPS-R035.1. | Test: `tests/api/test_resident_tool_surface.py::test_running_server_lists_exactly_the_resident_tools` (L3). It boots the server and lists tools over MCP, then asserts the middleware denies nothing. |
| `ARBORA-OPS-R036` | **Pre-dispatch audit reservation for governed effects.** Before any governed write or admin effect, Arbora reserves a tenant-scoped, idempotent pending audit record through the engine's audit-append operation using verified service claims, links the eventual outcome back to that reservation, and refuses dispatch when the operation has no declared audit class or the durable audit writer is unavailable. | Integration tests confirm a missing audit class or unreachable audit writer blocks dispatch and that a completed effect links to its reservation. |
| `ARBORA-OPS-R037` | **Native first-party A2A facade.** Arbora exposes a first-party, natively hosted A2A facade rather than proxying through an external bridge, serving card, send, get, list, and cancel operations directly from its own server process. | Integration tests exercise the native A2A endpoints without any external bridge dependency. |
| `ARBORA-OPS-R038` | **Generated OpenAPI served through Swagger UI.** Arbora generates an OpenAPI document from its operation registry and serves it through a browsable Swagger UI page. | An integration test confirms the served OpenAPI document matches the generated registry and renders through the Swagger UI endpoint. |

Verified by the root slice test. |
| `ARBORA-OPS-R042.2` | **Default screen rules and output redactor.** Stage: arbora invoke pipeline. Code: `arbora/api/invoke/screening.py` holds `default_screen_rules` and `default_output_redactor`, both registered by name in a `ScreenRegistry`. Rule: one default rule per attack class. Each matches hidden text (zero-width characters, CSS-hidden elements, HTML comments), rendered text instructions, or markup-embedded instructions aimed at that class. Findings in `memory`, `action`, `multi_agent` and `overseer` are `Fixed` quarantine classes. `perception` and `reasoning` findings are flagged and their spans removed. The redactor replaces these with `[REDACTED]`: bearer tokens, `sk-` style keys, cloud access keys and private-key blocks. It also masks values under keys named `password`, `secret` or `token`. Configuration: `content_screen.rules` and `content_screen.redactor` names in `~/.config/arbora/config.json`, defaults the two default names; `content_screen.max_scan_bytes` default 1048576, bounds `[4096, 16777216]`. Refusals: content above the scan limit is quarantined. Evidence: findings. Writes: none. Depends on: ARBORA-OPS-R042.1. | Test: `tests/api/test_content_screening.py::test_each_attack_class_and_masked_secret` (L1). It plants one case per class and one secret per pattern. |
| `ARBORA-OPS-R042.3` | **Screening wiring.** Stage: arbora invoke pipeline. Caller: `make_endpoint` in `arbora/api/http/routes.py` → `invoke` in `arbora/api/invoke/pipeline.py`. Code: `_effect` in `arbora/api/invoke/pipeline.py` calls `screen_result` on the result of an `open_web` operation before it returns. It calls `redact_result` on every result. For `memory.write` with `content_origin` set to `open_web` in its params, `invoke` calls `screen_params` before the effect. This row creates both call sites. Order: authority, screen of params, effect, screen of result, redaction, audit. Refusals: see ARBORA-OPS-R042.4. Evidence: see ARBORA-OPS-R042.5. Writes: none. Depends on: ARBORA-OPS-R042.2. | Test: `tests/api/test_content_screening.py::test_invoke_calls_screen_then_redactor_in_order` (L3). It records call order through the served route. |
| `ARBORA-OPS-R042.4` | **Screening refusals.** Stage: arbora invoke pipeline. Code: add `CONTENT_QUARANTINED` (HTTP 422) and `UNSCREENED_MEMORY_WRITE` (HTTP 422) to `ArboraErrorCode` in `arbora/api/errors.py`. Rule: a `quarantined` verdict returns `OpResult(code='CONTENT_QUARANTINED')` with the finding classes and no content. A long-term memory write with `content_origin` `open_web` returns `UNSCREENED_MEMORY_WRITE` unless the screen ran and the verdict is `clean`. A `flagged` verdict also refuses. The body-size refusal in `_json_body_params` of `arbora/api/http/routes.py` stays HTTP 413. Evidence: see ARBORA-OPS-R042.5. Writes: none. Depends on: ARBORA-OPS-R042.3. | Test: `tests/api/test_content_screening.py::test_quarantine_and_unscreened_memory_write_refuse_with_zero_effects` (L3). It asserts both codes and no memory write. |
| `ARBORA-OPS-R042.5` | **Screening evidence.** Stage: arbora invoke pipeline. Code: the audit record of `EgAuditAdapter` gains `screen_verdict`, `finding_classes`, `rule_ids` and `redaction_count`. Rule: the record never holds matched text or a redacted value. Evidence is written for every screened call, including refusals. Writes: the audit record only. Depends on: ARBORA-OPS-R042.3. | Test: `tests/api/test_content_screening.py::test_audit_holds_fields_and_no_secret_text` (L3). It asserts the four fields and the absence of the planted secret. |
| `ARBORA-OPS-R042.6` | **Screening hot-path proof.** Stage: arbora invoke pipeline. Entry point: `POST /api/v1/ops/research.web` over the served route in `arbora/api/http/routes.py`. The test plants hidden instructions in a recorded page and a secret in a clean response. It runs over HTTP and MCP with a loopback engine. Rule: the planted page returns `CONTENT_QUARANTINED`. The clean response returns with the secret masked. Writes: none. Depends on: ARBORA-OPS-R042.3, ARBORA-OPS-R042.4, ARBORA-OPS-R067. | Test: `tests/gateway/test_content_screening_hot_path.py::test_served_route_quarantines_hidden_instructions_and_masks_secrets` (L3). |

The following rows retain their original IDs, requirements and verification criteria. Each row is retired because its replacement is owned by this spec. Retirement does not establish acceptance of the replacement.

| `ARBORA-OPS-R051.1` | **Security evaluation model.** Stage: arbora served operations. Code: new `arbora/api/ops/security_eval.py` with frozen Pydantic models `EvaluationGoal{text: str(1..4096)}`, `EvaluationCatalog{entries: tuple[CatalogEntry]}`, `EvaluationScope{target_ids: tuple[str], max_probes: int}` and `Finding{probe_id, category, verdict, evidence_ref}`. `verdict` is the closed set `resisted`, `compromised`, `inconclusive` of MEDULLA-DECISION-ENGINE-R138.1. `SecurityPolicyMiddleware` in `arbora/security/security_policy_middleware.py` keeps its input jailbreak detection and is unrelated. | `ARBORA-OPS-R051.2` | **Security evaluation behavior.** Stage: arbora served operations. Caller: `handle_security_evaluate` → `SecurityEvaluationPort`. Rule: (1) validate the target against the caller's authorized scope. (2) ask the engine for catalog selection (`Decide`, MEDULLA-DECISION-ENGINE-R818). (3) send the selected probes to the harness through the `SecurityProbeRunner` port, whose production adapter submits the existing `agents.run` operation. (4) send each probe result to `Decide` with `QuestionKind::ProbeVerdict` (MEDULLA-DECISION-ENGINE-R138.3). (5) return the findings. The probe cap is the engine value `knob red_team.max_probes` (MEDULLA-DECISION-ENGINE-R133 quota). The fallback default when the decision is absent is 50. Arbora composes and scores nothing locally. Refusals: see ARBORA-OPS-R051.4. Evidence: see ARBORA-OPS-R051.5. Writes: none beyond the audit record. Depends on: ARBORA-OPS-R051.1, MEDULLA-DECISION-ENGINE-R818. | Test: `tests/api/test_security_eval_select.py::test_only_declared_catalog_entries_are_selected_and_cap_applies` (L2). It asserts selection comes from the engine answer and the cap holds. |

| `ARBORA-OPS-R053` | **Bounded interaction space (rollup).** Subsystem: arbora served operation `retrieval.space`. It returns a bounded interaction space, not ranked snippets. The corpus boundary is the union of the top-K hits of a multi-query lexical search, with one directory per query. Medulla owns ranking (MEDULLA-FEDERATED-QUERY-R001), retrieval-plan decisions (MEDULLA-DECISION-ENGINE-R029) and context budgets (MEDULLA-DECISION-ENGINE-R137). The harness executes the engine-directed exploration (MERISTEM-HARNESS-CYCLE-R031). Caller: `POST /api/v1/ops/retrieval.space` → `make_endpoint` in `arbora/api/http/routes.py` → `invoke` in `arbora/api/invoke/pipeline.py` → `handle_retrieval_space` in `arbora/api/ops/retrieval.py`. `retrieval.search` stays with ARBORA-OPS-R021.2. Children: `.1` model to `.6` hot-path proof. Indexless scans and line tables are served by MEDULLA-DECISION-ENGINE-R144 and MEDULLA-DECISION-ENGINE-R153 (ARBORA-OPS-R053.7 and R053.8 are retired). Depends on: ARBORA-OPS-R021.2. | Root L3 journey: `tests/api/test_retrieval_space_served.py::test_two_queries_yield_boundary_and_two_directories` (L3). |
| `ARBORA-OPS-R053.1` | **Interaction space model.** Stage: arbora served operations. Code: add to `arbora/api/ops/retrieval.py` the frozen models `CorpusBoundary{document_refs, top_k}`, `QueryDirectory{name, query, hit_refs, properties, commands, toc_digests}` and `InteractionSpace{space_id, boundary, directories}`. The `Retriever` port returns hits only. These models add the directory layout and the property documentation. `properties` maps each object property name to a one-line description; `commands` lists the navigation commands. Refusals: `INVALID_ARGUMENT`. Writes: none. Depends on: none. | Test: `tests/api/test_interaction_space_models.py::test_models_validate_fields` (L1). Verified by the root slice test. |
| `ARBORA-OPS-R053.2` | **Interaction space build.** Stage: arbora served operations. Caller: `handle_retrieval_space` → `build_space(outcomes, plan)` in `arbora/api/ops/retrieval.py`. Rule: the boundary is the union of the top-K hit references over all queries, in first-seen order. One directory per query is named `q01`, `q02` in query order. K and the query cap come from the retrieval-plan decision (MEDULLA-DECISION-ENGINE-R029): `knob retrieval_space.top_k` and `knob retrieval_space.max_queries`. The fallback defaults, used only when the decision is absent, are 20 and 8. The boundary size ceiling is the engine context budget (MEDULLA-DECISION-ENGINE-R137). Arbora ranks nothing. Refusals: see ARBORA-OPS-R053.4. Evidence: see ARBORA-OPS-R053.5. Writes: none. Depends on: ARBORA-OPS-R053.1, MEDULLA-DECISION-ENGINE-R029. | Test: `tests/api/test_interaction_space_build.py::test_union_caps_and_directory_names` (L2). |
| `ARBORA-OPS-R053.3` | **Interaction space wiring.** Stage: arbora served operations. Code: register `retrieval.space` beside `handle_retrieval_search` in `operations()` of `arbora/api/ops/retrieval.py`, built with `build_read_op`, scope `memory:read`. The handler calls `GovernedRetriever.retrieve` in `arbora/control_plane/retrieval/service.py`. No production constructor of `GovernedRetriever` exists. Add `_with_retriever` in `arbora/mcp_server/served_invocation.py`, called from `compose_served_invocation` in `arbora/mcp_server/served_invocation.py`. It builds the engine-backed retriever and binds `retriever`, which `retrieval.search` also uses. Refusals: `UNAVAILABLE` when no retriever is bound. Writes: none. Depends on: ARBORA-OPS-R053.2. | Test: `tests/api/test_interaction_space_wiring.py::test_served_request_reaches_composed_retriever_and_missing_producer_refuses` (L3). |
| `ARBORA-OPS-R053.4` | **Interaction space refusals.** Stage: arbora served operations. Rule: a request whose boundary exceeds the engine document ceiling returns `LOAD_CAP_EXCEEDED`. An empty query list returns `INVALID_ARGUMENT`. A retriever that is not composed returns `UNAVAILABLE`. No retrieval runs after a refusal. Writes: none. Depends on: ARBORA-OPS-R053.3. | Test: `tests/api/test_interaction_space_refusal.py::test_three_refusals_run_zero_queries` (L3). |
| `ARBORA-OPS-R053.5` | **Interaction space evidence.** Stage: arbora served operations. Rule: the audit record holds the query count, the boundary size, the per-query hit counts and the context budget used. Writes: the audit record. Depends on: ARBORA-OPS-R053.3. | Test: `tests/api/test_interaction_space_evidence.py::test_audit_record_fields` (L3). |
| `ARBORA-OPS-R053.6` | **Interaction space hot-path proof.** Stage: arbora served operations. Entry point: `POST /api/v1/ops/retrieval.space`. The test runs two queries against a loopback engine and observes the boundary and two directories. Missing producer contracts stay typed `UNAVAILABLE`. Writes: none. Depends on: ARBORA-OPS-R053.3. | Test: `tests/api/test_retrieval_space_served.py::test_two_queries_yield_boundary_and_two_directories` (L3). |
| `ARBORA-OPS-R054` | **Technique-bound benchmark adoption (rollup).** Subsystem: arbora served operation `serving.technique.activate` over the existing `decide.commit` write path. The five techniques are `paged_cache`, `prefix_reuse`, `fused_kernels`, `quantized_cache` and `expert_dispatch`. The harness emits benchmark receipts (MERISTEM-HARNESS-CYCLE-R062). Medulla decides activation from the receipts (MEDULLA-DECISION-ENGINE-R819). Arbora serves and applies only the committed verdict. `handle_rlm` in `arbora/api/ops/agents.py` serves `rlm.benchmark`, which is not technique evidence. Caller: `POST /api/v1/ops/serving.technique.activate` → `make_endpoint` in `arbora/api/http/routes.py` → `invoke` in `arbora/api/invoke/pipeline.py` → `handle_technique_activate` in new `arbora/api/ops/serving_techniques.py`. Children: `.1` model and behavior, `.2` wiring, refusals and proof. Depends on: MEDULLA-DECISION-ENGINE-R819, MERISTEM-HARNESS-CYCLE-R062. | Root L3 journey: `tests/api/test_serving_technique_served.py::test_activation_requires_technique_bound_evidence` (L3). |
| `ARBORA-OPS-R055` | **Run-bound process remediation (rollup).** Subsystem: arbora served operations `ops.process.propose` (read) and `ops.process.terminate` (`Effect.ADMIN`, `Confirm.PLAN`). The harness emits liveness evidence and stops on request (MERISTEM-HARNESS-CYCLE-R063). Medulla decides zombie or hung status (MEDULLA-DECISION-ENGINE-R820). Quota is not authority. Caller: `POST /api/v1/ops/ops.process.propose` → `make_endpoint` in `arbora/api/http/routes.py` → `invoke` in `arbora/api/invoke/pipeline.py` → `handle_process_propose` in `arbora/api/ops/ops.py`. Children: `.1` proposal, `.2` stop and proof. Depends on: MEDULLA-DECISION-ENGINE-R820, MERISTEM-HARNESS-CYCLE-R063. | Root L3 journey: `tests/api/test_ops_admin_ops.py::test_process_remediation_requires_run_bound_approval` (L3). |
| `ARBORA-OPS-R056` | **RETIRED: served by the changeset review operation.** ARBORA-OPS-R870 serves `review.changeset`, which calls `Decide` with `QuestionKind::ChangesetReview`. The revision binding, evidence rule and impact filter move there. | Retired; see ARBORA-OPS-R870. |
| `ARBORA-OPS-R057` | **RETIRED: owned by the engine contract and governed write-back.** MEDULLA-CONTRACT-R001.2 owns the canonical prompt disk round trip. SCION-GOVERNED-WRITEBACK-R004.5 owns repository source effects. Arbora serves them as `eg.*` and `source.*` operations. | Retired; see MEDULLA-CONTRACT-R001.2. |
| `ARBORA-OPS-R058` | **RETIRED: served by the query operations and schema context.** ARBORA-OPS-R018.2 serves authorized table reads through `query.uql` and `query.sql`. ARBORA-DATA-MARKET-R005 serves `schema_context`. No separate table-browser operation exists. | Retired; see ARBORA-OPS-R018.2. |
| `ARBORA-OPS-R061` | **Indexed token and cost projection (rollup).** Subsystem: arbora served operation `usage.index.query`. It forwards the trace usage query of MEDULLA-ORCH-VOCAB-R010.101. Arbora performs no token or cost arithmetic. Caller: `POST /api/v1/ops/usage.index.query` or MCP → `make_endpoint` in `arbora/api/http/routes.py` → `invoke` in `arbora/api/invoke/pipeline.py` → `handle_usage_index_query` in `arbora/api/ops/usage.py` → `UsageIndexReader.query`. Consumer: OVERSTORY-APPS-R004.8.1 to R004.8.6 through `ObservabilityView.tsx`. Preserved: `runs.metrics` grouping (ARBORA-OPS-R021.4.3, ARBORA-OPS-R021.4.4); `usage.read` context peaks and budgets are not total usage. Children: `.1` models, `.2` forwarding, `.3` wiring, `.4` refusals and latency profile, `.5` proof. Producer: `MEDULLA-ORCH-VOCAB-R010.101`. The types `RunTraceUsageQuery` and `PriceTable` carry no version suffix. Depends on: MEDULLA-ORCH-VOCAB-R010.101. | Root L3 journey: `tests/api/test_usage_index_served.py::test_browser_query_reconciles_with_independent_ledger` (L3). |
| `ARBORA-OPS-R063` | **Retrieval evaluation and authorized context projection (rollup).** Subsystem: arbora served operations `retrieval.evaluation.compare` and `retrieval.context.search`. They forward `RetrievalRankingSubmit` (MEDULLA-DECISION-ENGINE-R100.100.2, served by `DecisionEval`) and `Method::RetrievalContextSearch` (MEDULLA-DECISION-ENGINE-R100.100.4). Arbora never computes `hits_at_k` and never treats local fusion as relevance truth. Caller: `POST /api/v1/ops/retrieval.evaluation.compare` → `make_endpoint` in `arbora/api/http/routes.py` → `invoke` in `arbora/api/invoke/pipeline.py` → `handle_retrieval_compare` in `arbora/api/ops/retrieval.py`. Consumer: OVERSTORY-APPS-R001.1.1 to R001.1.6 through `TryItPanel.tsx`. Children: `.1` models, `.2` evaluation, `.3` context search, `.4` refusals, `.5` proof. Depends on: MEDULLA-DECISION-ENGINE-R100.100. | Root L3 journey: `tests/api/test_retrieval_evaluation_served.py::test_two_algorithms_match_independent_hits_at_k_ledger` (L3). |

| `ARBORA-OPS-R022.4` | **AU:53 Arbora definition slice.** AU:53 cross-owner slice. Residual: The contract does not define grading every output or converting checker findings into the next instruction. It also omits durable continuation until no fixes remain or a bound is reached. A once-per-evaluation-round gate plus a run API does not establish that feedback loop. Arbora minimum action: Extend the existing runner/evaluation loop contract with every-output checker feedback, findings-to-next-instruction. Durable continuation and no-fixes/bound stopping rules; preserve R022 fail-closed scope/idempotency criteria. Producer/consumer boundary: AU triggered runner/output -> checker/evaluation report -> EG authority and durable state. Arbora exposes the loop status/run/pause facade. The report-to-next-instruction and stopping links remain undefined. External engine, SDK, AU, Pipelines or WebUI behavior remains owned there; this row specifies only Arbora. Current IDs: `ARBORA-OPS-R022, MERISTEM-HARNESS-R021, MERISTEM-HARNESS-R003`. Source record SHA-256: `3432e0278c2f92fce06790822ed67bd032678dcf58ab47c2a76934dfd9356199`. Reviewed evidence paths: `spec-audit/reconciliation/fresh-validation-20261012/semantic-reviews/incoming-24-owner-supplements.json, spec-audit/writer-queue-merged/agent-utilities.tsv:53, meristem/specs/harness-evolution/requirements.md:7#sha256=647388f1d7122d54ada7d05db4010abd8cd64d7677c5be11f5a4e2c4ddbcf85e, meristem/specs/harness-evolution/requirements.md:100#sha256=73eb8c3e4b8fa8e15715730c8a52dc5c6c48048eabb1bdb3f7ef422b2d895a65, arbora/specs/hosted-api-operations/requirements.md:52#sha256=00a3f51ddcde9bedb3edbbe07293f9b7610e10212e9be4f93df2b7809386f6b7, meristem/specs/harness-evolution/requirements.md:105#sha256=17fc83fc314533c54b52e10691f758e762c25ecdc14e5897ced05993520446ee, meristem/specs/harness-evolution/requirements.md:107#sha256=70526a562b833c993d0b0dd5888d871afd291a21ae6bdac4cb35a90669081ed8, arbora/specs/hosted-api-operations/requirements.md:54#sha256=d8c69732119b9efd110bc34158c3782be3ac3914844a8564936c35fe22dcbe9f`. | `ARBORA-OPS-R053.7` | **RETIRED: served by the engine indexless method.** MEDULLA-DECISION-ENGINE-R144.3 serves `Method::IndexlessRetrieve` as an `eg.*` operation with budget and cost reporting. MERISTEM-HARNESS-CYCLE-R031 stops exploration on exhausted budget. | Retired; see MEDULLA-DECISION-ENGINE-R144. |
| `ARBORA-OPS-R021.2.1.1` | **RETIRED: bound retrieval provider execution has one owner.** `ARBORA-OPS-R864` makes `retrieval.search` run only the bound provider. `MEDULLA-DECISION-ENGINE-R029.1` makes the binding. | Retired; see ARBORA-OPS-R864. |
| `ARBORA-OPS-R050.2.1` | **Bind a reviewed policy source to the active policy.** Subsystem: policy operations. Caller: `handle_policy_change` → `arbora/api/ops/policy.py::PolicyActivationPort.bind_source` (new) → served operation `eg.graph.PolicySourceBind`. After a human review, arbora sends `source_ref`, `source_revision`, `source_digest`, `policy_iri` and `approver`. The values come from the review receipt of `ARBORA-OPS-R071`. The engine side is `MEDULLA-SRC-AUTH-R801`. A rejected or stale review sends nothing. The engine refusals `POLICY_SOURCE_DIGEST_MISMATCH`, `POLICY_SOURCE_STALE` and `POLICY_SOURCE_UNREVIEWED` pass through unchanged. Each leaves the file and the active policy unchanged. Arbora never writes the source file. A source change uses governed write-back with approval. Writes: none in arbora. Depends on: `MEDULLA-SRC-AUTH-R801`, `ARBORA-OPS-R071`, `ARBORA-OPS-R050.2`. | Test: `tests/api/test_policy_source_bind_served.py::test_bind_sends_receipt_fields_and_stale_review_leaves_policy_unchanged` (L3). It calls the registry over HTTP and MCP with a recorded engine. It asserts the sent fields, the three refusals and zero source writes. |
| `ARBORA-OPS-R009.1` | **RETIRED: recommendation approval and decision-wide evidence have one owner per side.** `ARBORA-OPS-R863` issues the approval receipt and `ARBORA-OPS-R862` gates surfacing. `MEDULLA-DECISION-ENGINE-R019.100` seals the evidence streams and checks the receipt. | Retired; see ARBORA-OPS-R863. |
| `ARBORA-OPS-R009.2` | **Refusal causes are logged.** The invoke pipeline and the error envelope must log the internal cause of an `UNAVAILABLE`, `INTERNAL` or `INDETERMINATE` outcome server-side. The log names the operation, the request ID and the exception. The client envelope stays redacted and carries the same request ID. A provider-schema refusal logs its first validation message, bounded to 300 characters. An engine refusal logs the engine code and its bounded diagnostic detail. | `tests/api/test_refusal_causes.py::test_refusal_cause_is_logged_with_request_id` observes the logged cause and the redacted envelope. |
| `ARBORA-OPS-R009.3` | **Declared replay owners.** An ops module may export `NATIVE_IDEMPOTENCY`: operation ID to replay owner. The invoke pipeline must collect every module's declaration. A governed write without a declared owner or the effect journal answers `UNAVAILABLE` with reason `durable effect owner unavailable`. | `tests/api/test_refusal_causes.py::test_ops_modules_declare_replay_owners` finds `agents.run` and a test module's declaration. |
| `ARBORA-OPS-R009.4` | **A typed refusal names its reason, never its input.** An `INVALID_ARGUMENT` envelope keeps `field`, the field-name lists `fields`, `unexpected`, `missing`, `invalid` and `required`, and an Arbora-authored `reason`. Each value is bounded, and the envelope drops every other key. An input that misses its engine contract schema names its unexpected, missing and invalid fields, never a value. An engine refusal keeps its detail text in the operator log. The envelope gets only the first upper-snake reason code and its `at line:column` position. Arbora refuses a connector prompt call that omits a declared required argument. The `INVALID_ARGUMENT` refusal names the missing arguments, and arbora sends nothing to the child. A child that reports missing prompt arguments gets the same refusal. Any other child prompt failure is the child's own `CHILD_REFUSED` with its redacted detail, never `INTERNAL`. Validation run 2026-10-24 gaps G14 and G10: `query.uql` with `query` in place of `text` was refused with empty details. `create_branch_prompt` without arguments answered `INTERNAL`. | `tests/api/test_typed_refusal_details.py` drives the production registry through the invoke pipeline and the envelope. `tests/fleet/test_relay_session_recovery.py::test_a_prompt_called_without_its_arguments_is_a_typed_refusal` renders a real FastMCP child prompt; `::test_declared_required_prompt_arguments_are_checked_before_the_child`. |
| `ARBORA-OPS-R009.5` | **Forward the approval reference to connector calls.** Caller: registered destructive operation → `arbora/api/invoke/pipeline.py::invoke` → `arbora/api/invoke/executor.py::BoundOperationRuntime.dispatch`. After the approval check of ARBORA-OPS-R015, the pipeline sets `_meta["arbora/approval_ref"]` on the connector `tools/call`. It refuses denied, expired and wrong-operation approvals with the closed error enum before the call. Consumer: SCION-CONNECTOR-CONTROL-R050.4. | Test: `tests/api/test_connector_approval_forward.py::test_destructive_call_carries_approval_ref` (L3) through the registry `invoke` over HTTP and MCP. |
| `ARBORA-OPS-R009.6` | **Operator hook model and configuration.** Stage: invoke pipeline bootstrap. Caller: host bootstrap → `arbora/api/invoke/bootstrap.py` → `load_hooks` in new `arbora/api/invoke/hooks.py`. The new module is reached from `InvokeServices` in `arbora/api/invoke/pipeline.py`, which gains the field `hooks: HookChain`. Types: `HookPoint` (`input`, `output`), `HookSpec { name, point, op_ref }`, `HookDecision` (a closed union of `allow`, `redact { spans }` and `reject { code }`) and `Span { path, start, end }`. Configuration is the file `operator_hooks.json` under `config_dir()` of `arbora/core/settings.py`, key `hooks`: an ordered list of `HookSpec`. A hook is a reference to a registered operation. Arbora loads no code from the file. Bootstrap validation refuses a hook in three cases. Its `op_ref` is absent from the registry. Its operation has an effect other than `READ`. Its operation is another hook. A refusal stops the host start with the reason `hook_invalid`. Hooks run in declared order. Knob `operator_hooks.timeout_s` is `Fixed` at 2 seconds. Writes: none. Depends on: `ARBORA-OPS-R009`. Shape: one new file `hooks.py` holding the types, `load_hooks` and `HookChain`; validate through one table of rules, and reuse `Registry.get`. | Test: `tests/api/test_operator_hooks.py::test_bootstrap_refuses_absent_write_or_recursive_hook` (module test). Test: `tests/api/test_operator_hooks.py::test_hooks_load_in_declared_order` (module test). Hot-path proof: `ARBORA-OPS-R009.9`. |
| `ARBORA-OPS-R009.7` | **Input hook runs before the input is admitted.** Stage: invoke pipeline, after `_resolve` and before `_confirmation_gate` in `invoke` (`arbora/api/invoke/pipeline.py`). Caller: the served route → `invoke` → `HookChain.run_input` in `arbora/api/invoke/hooks.py`. The chain calls each `input` hook with `{op_id, tenant, params}`. It invokes the hook operation through `invoke` itself with the host service principal, the flag `hooks_enabled` false and the same `request_id`. The hook sees the caller's tenant and never sees a credential. `allow` passes the params on. `redact` replaces each named span of a string param by the fixed text `[redacted]` and passes the result on. `reject` ends the call with `OpError(code)`, where `code` is the hook's code or `HOOK_REJECTED`. The next hook receives the output of the previous hook. A redacted param set must still pass `_arguments` validation. Writes: none. Depends on: `ARBORA-OPS-R009.6`, `ARBORA-OPS-R042.3`. Shape: one method `run_input(request: HookRequest)` that dispatches through a table from decision kind to an apply function; reuse the span masker of `arbora/security/log_redaction.py`. | Test: `tests/api/test_operator_hooks.py::test_input_hook_allow_redact_and_reject` (served: HTTP and MCP through the registry `invoke`) asserts the three decisions and the order of two hooks. |
| `ARBORA-OPS-R009.8` | **Output hook runs before a response leaves.** Stage: invoke pipeline, in `_execute` after `_dispatch_outcome` and before `_record` (`arbora/api/invoke/pipeline.py`). Caller: the served route → `invoke` → `_execute` → `HookChain.run_output`. Each `output` hook receives `{op_id, tenant, result}`. The three decisions apply as in `ARBORA-OPS-R009.7`. The outcome after the hooks is the outcome that `_record` writes to the effect journal. A replay therefore returns the same response. A `reject` at the output point replaces the result by `OpError(code)`. The effect has already happened, and the audit record states this with `effect_applied` true. An `OpError` result passes the output hook only when the hook has the flag `include_errors`. The default is false. Writes: none. Depends on: `ARBORA-OPS-R009.7`, `ARBORA-OPS-R042.3`. Shape: reuse the apply table of `run_input`; add the call as one line in `_execute`. | Test: `tests/api/test_operator_hooks.py::test_output_hook_redacts_and_rejects_and_replay_matches` (served: HTTP and MCP) asserts the replayed response equals the hooked response. |
| `ARBORA-OPS-R009.9` | **Hooks fail closed and record their decisions.** Stage: invoke pipeline. A hook that raises, returns an unknown decision or exceeds `operator_hooks.timeout_s` (`Fixed`, 2 seconds) rejects the call. The code is `HOOK_FAILED`. The envelope names the hook and holds no hook output. No later hook runs. The effect journal receives the rejection like any other outcome. `audit_event` in `arbora/api/invoke/audit.py` gains the field `hooks`, a list of `{name, point, decision, duration_ms}`. The list holds no matched text. The same list enters the run trace when the call belongs to a run: `arbora/api/ops/run_graph.py` reads it from the audit event. Add `HOOK_FAILED` and `HOOK_REJECTED` to `arbora/api/errors.py`. Writes: the audit event only. Depends on: `ARBORA-OPS-R009.7`, `ARBORA-OPS-R009.8`. Shape: one `asyncio.wait_for` wrapper in `hooks.py`; reuse the `_typed_refusal` path for the envelope. | Test: `tests/api/test_operator_hooks.py::test_timeout_error_and_unknown_decision_reject_with_hook_failed` (served) and `::test_decisions_are_recorded_without_matched_text` (served). Hot-path proof: `tests/api/test_operator_hooks.py::test_two_hooks_through_http_and_mcp` enters through the HTTP route and the MCP tool. |

| `ARBORA-OPS-R067.1` | **Model.** Subsystem: research operations. `ResearchWebParams` and `ResearchWebResult` live in `arbora/api/ops/research.py`. They are not moved. Params are `query`, `urls`, `max_pages`, `backend`, `freshness_window_s` and `deadline_s`. Result fields are `work_item_id`, `decision_id`, `state`, `documents`, `skipped`, `fallbacks`, `ingest` and `embedding`. Bounds: `max_pages` 1 to 32, default 5. `deadline_s` 1 to 300 s, default 60. `freshness_window_s` 0 to 31,536,000 s, default 86,400. `backend` is `webarchiver` or `archivebox`, default `webarchiver`. A call must name `query` or 1 to 32 `urls`. `state` is `complete`, `partial` or `enriching`. A document entry holds the Document IRI and one citation per chunk. A citation names the URL, retrieval time, content digest, backend, query and rank. Pydantic rejects extra fields. Depends on: `ARBORA-OPS-R067.2`. | Test: `tests/api/test_research_ops.py::test_params_and_result_round_trip` (unit). It asserts a round trip keeps every field and refuses 33 pages and a call with neither query nor URLs. |
| `ARBORA-OPS-R067.2` | **Behavior.** Subsystem: research operations. Caller: served `invoke` (`arbora/api/invoke/pipeline.py::invoke`) → `arbora/api/ops/research.py::handle_research_web` → `EgEnrich.select`. Step 1: a `query` call asks the engine web metasearch for one `WebCandidateSet` (`MEDULLA-UNIFIED-DATA-PLANE-R038.7.7`). A `urls` call skips this step and uses the URLs as candidates, in order. Step 2: the handler submits one engine work item with `arbora.work_items.submit_work_item_atomic`. The kind is `research_web`. The idempotency key is the query digest plus caller tenant. Step 3: it sends the candidates to the served `SelectWebPages` method, operation `eg.query.SelectWebPages` (`MEDULLA-DECISION-ENGINE-R802.3`) and fetches only the selected URLs. Step 4: after `SourceIngest`, it calls engine enrichment, operation `eg.ingestion.EnrichDocument` (`MEDULLA-UNIFIED-DATA-PLANE-R651.7`), for each stored Document. The work item commits with `arbora.work_items.commit_result` when every selected page has settled. Stop fetching at `deadline_s`. Remaining pages become skips with reason `deadline` and `state` is `partial`. `state` is `enriching` only when enrichment has not finished at the deadline. The caller then reads the work item through `ingest.jobs.status` (`ARBORA-OPS-R020.3.1.2`). Delete `InterimEnrich` and its stand-ins. `EgEnrich` binds to the engine client only. Depends on: `ARBORA-OPS-R067.1`, `MEDULLA-DECISION-ENGINE-R802.3`, `MEDULLA-UNIFIED-DATA-PLANE-R651.7.1`, MEDULLA-UNIFIED-DATA-PLANE-R766. | Test: `tests/api/test_research_ops.py::test_composes_four_steps_in_order` (served). The recording engine sees the search, the work item, the selection and the enrichment in that order. `test_deadline_returns_partial_or_enriching` asserts both states. |
| `ARBORA-OPS-R067.3` | **Backend choice and fallback.** `webarchiver` is the primary backend. `archivebox` is the secondary backend. A caller may select it explicitly with `backend=archivebox`. When the chosen backend refuses a page with `upstream_failed`, is unreachable or times out (`ARBORA-OPS-R067.9`), arbora must fetch that page once more with the other backend. arbora must not fall back after a policy refusal such as `robots_disallowed` or `policy_refused`. Both backends must produce identical ingest output: `SourceIngest`, then engine enrichment (`MEDULLA-UNIFIED-DATA-PLANE-R651.7`), then citations. Each citation and each provenance record must name the backend that produced the page. Proving tests: `tests/api/test_research_web_backends.py::test_explicit_archivebox_run` and `tests/api/test_research_web_backends.py::test_forced_fallback_run`. | A `backend=archivebox` run and a forced-fallback run each return Documents equal to the primary run, except for the recorded backend. |

Keep both. The handler reads the ingest writer from `context.services['web_ingest']` (port `WebIngest`, default `SourceIngestWriter`) and the engine from `context.client`. An absent engine client refuses `UNAVAILABLE`. Titles and snippets in the result pass the open-web screen that `invoke` runs (`ARBORA-OPS-R042.3`). The handler adds no screen of its own. | `ARBORA-OPS-R067.5` | **Refusal.** Subsystem: research operations. Caller: served `invoke` (`arbora/api/invoke/pipeline.py::invoke`) → `handle_research_web`. Refusals use the closed Arbora codes of `ArboraErrorCode`. A caller without `ingest:write` receives `SCOPE_REQUIRED`. An engine abstention from `SelectWebPages` passes through with its code (`WEB_SELECTION_QUERY_MISSING`, `WEB_SELECTION_NO_CANDIDATES`, `WEB_SELECTION_TOO_MANY_CANDIDATES`, `WEB_SELECTION_BUDGET_INVALID`). It enqueues no fetch. An unreachable engine returns `UNAVAILABLE` with reason `decision_unavailable`. An unknown backend returns `INVALID_ARGUMENT` with field `backend`. An absent ingest writer returns `UNAVAILABLE` with reason `ingest_unavailable`. Arbora never ranks, fetches or ingests on its own after a refusal. | `ARBORA-OPS-R067.9` | **Bounded page fetch.** Each `web_extract` call has a bound of `PAGE_TIMEOUT_S` (30 s). The bound never runs past the operation `deadline_s`. A backend that does not answer within the bound fails the page with reason `timeout`. That reason is fallback-eligible under `ARBORA-OPS-R067.3`. When both backends time out, the page is a `timeout` skip, and the operation still answers within its deadline. | `tests/api/test_research_web_backends.py::test_hanging_archivebox_times_out_and_falls_back` returns a webarchiver page with `fallback_from: timeout` when archivebox never answers. `test_both_backends_hanging_skip_within_the_bound` returns a `timeout` skip. `test_page_timeout_never_passes_the_deadline` caps the bound at the remaining deadline. |
